Leads: EU Directive 2002/44/EC action value 2.5 m/s² A(8), limit value 5 m/s² A(8) (not a US OSHA numeric standard; ACGIH TLV is the closer US reference and needs its own check); a high-vibration breaker can reach the action value in about 15 minutes of trigger time.

### Solo-Work Safety: Tasks with a Never-Alone Consensus

## Inspection and Approval Sequence

1. OAR 918-008-0120 requires choosing one appeal route, the local appeals board or the state specialty code chief, at the outset, with no switching after starting. What factors should drive that choice for a novel-material dispute (speed, the reviewer's technical sophistication, cost, precedent value), and has any owner-builder documented choosing wrong?
Lands in: Inspection and Approval Sequence. [CODE]
(Attorney-grade: the irreversible choice has real consequences and deserves a construction attorney's input before filing.)

2. What are the exact qualification requirements for local board-of-appeals members under ORSC R112 as adopted in Oregon, including whether R112.4's Administration language is adopted or struck, and does a rural county's board typically include anyone with earthen, masonry, or alternative-materials expertise, or only conventional-framing experience?
Lands in: Inspection and Approval Sequence. [CODE]
Leads: one secondary source suggested R112.4 may not be adopted by Oregon BCD as part of the state code; needs direct primary-text confirmation.

3. Beyond Portland's specific model (three members and three alternates, mayor-appointed, one builder and one architect minimum, a first-tier Administrative Appeals Board before the full board), what does a rural county's board of appeals look like: how many members, how are they recruited in a small county, and how often does the board meet given that a board with no standing caseload may need weeks to convene for a first case?
Lands in: Inspection and Approval Sequence. [REAL-WORLD-EXPERIENCE] [CODE]

4. What does a complete, effective local board-of-appeals submission package look like for a technical structural-materials dispute: what exhibits, in what order, how much advance notice, and is oral testimony from the owner's engineer normally allowed?
Lands in: Inspection and Approval Sequence. [METHOD] [REAL-WORLD-EXPERIENCE]

5. Practically, what is the full realistic cost of a local board-of-appeals process for a contested alternative-materials denial, including the engineer's or attorney's time to prepare the package, beyond any filing fee the board itself charges?
Lands in: Inspection and Approval Sequence. [COST]
(Attorney-grade.)

6. Does filing a local appeal or a state appeal toll or pause the ORSC R105.5 permit-expiration clock or the ORS 455.467 plan-review timeline, or does the schedule keep running during a dispute?
Lands in: Inspection and Approval Sequence. [CODE]

7. What happens procedurally on a stop-work order under ORSC R114 issued mid-project over a disputed alternative material: can work continue on unrelated, undisputed parts of the house while the specific disputed item is litigated, or does a stop-work order freeze the entire jobsite?

8. Does the existing Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control chapter, or any other existing chapter, already assert that special inspection is a live ORSC requirement, and if so does that need correcting given that special inspection is confirmed to be principally an IBC Chapter 17 and OSSC concept largely inapplicable to detached one- and two-family dwellings?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [CODE]

## Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control

### Dry-Cast, Zero-Slump, and Static Pressing

1. How is "green strength" (the strength of a just-demolded, uncured unit) defined and measured, and what minimum green strength lets fresh units be stacked immediately without slumping or edge-crushing?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [METHOD]

### Cement-Stabilized Rammed Earth (CSRE)

2. For CSRE, what specimen geometry should the owner press and report (molded cylinder, cube, or core), and what conversion factors reconcile those to a C39-style value a building official will recognize?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [CODE]
Leads: 100x200 mm cylinder is approximately 0.80x the strength of a 150 mm cube; ASTM D1633 Method A (H/D 1.15) vs Method B (H/D 2.0).

3. What is the exact test-and-permit path for CSRE units: is the governing document ASTM D1633 (soil-cement cylinders), ASTM C39, or the New Mexico-style earthen-building strength protocol, and which will an Oregon building official accept under the alternative-materials clause?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [CODE]
Leads: NM Admin Code 14.7.4.23 sets 300 psi min, tested flat, loaded at 500 psi/min; D1633 is the soil-cement cylinder standard.

4. How durable is CSRE in wet western/central Oregon: wet/dry cycling, freeze/thaw, and rain erosion, and what minimum cement content or surface treatment is needed for durability, which may exceed the content needed for strength alone?
Lands in: Owner-Made Blocks and Alternative Concretes. [PROBLEM-SOLUTION] [TEST-TO-PROVE]
Leads: ASTM D559 (wet-dry) and D560 (freeze-thaw) soil-cement durability tests; durability often governs cement dose over strength.

### Compressed Earth Block and CSEB

5. What cement content and soil mix do CSEB recipes use to reach a target strength, and how much does the specimen capping method alone swing the measured result?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [MATERIALS/INGREDIENTS] [TEST-TO-PROVE]
Leads: same CSEB blocks tested ~6.09 MPa capped with plywood vs ~4.22 MPa capped with rubber, a ~30% difference from capping choice alone.

### Ferrocement

6. How is ferrocement's capacity proven for permitting when it is thin and mesh-reinforced (flexural/panel tests and mesh specifications rather than a compression cylinder), and what standard governs?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [CODE]

### Sulfur Concrete

7. How durable is sulfur concrete under Oregon wet/freeze-thaw and thermal cycling (its weakness), what temperature ceiling limits its use, and would a building official accept it structurally?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [PROBLEM-SOLUTION] [CODE]

### The Scientific Test-to-Prove Path and the R104.11 Permitting File

8. What exactly must an alternative-materials submission under ORSC R104.11 contain, what counts as an "approved agency," and who pays for the testing?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [CODE] [TEST-TO-PROVE]
Leads: R104.11.1: the building official may require tests as evidence, performed by an approved agency, at no expense to the municipality; where no recognized test exists the official approves the procedure.

9. Which ASTM test governs which material for the permit file: C39 cylinders for castable concretes, C140/C90 for units, C1314 prisms for wall systems, D1633 for soil-cement, and what is the decision tree an owner follows?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [CODE]

10. How much does specimen preparation change the reported number: sulfur capping (C617) versus neoprene pad caps (C1231) versus plywood/rubber on earthen units, and how does the owner avoid under-reporting his own material's strength?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [PROBLEM-SOLUTION]
Leads: same CSEB block read ~30% lower with rubber vs plywood caps; capping is a documented confounder.

11. How many specimens, and what statistical treatment (characteristic strength, coefficient of variation, safety factor), will a building official expect before accepting an owner-made material as loadbearing?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [CODE]
Leads: "characteristic compressive strength" framing used in CSRE literature.

12. Where are accredited (e.g., AASHTO/A2LA) compression-testing labs relative to rural Oregon, what do they charge per specimen, and will they test unconventional owner-made units or witness the owner's own press?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [COST] [TOOLS] [PROBLEM-SOLUTION]

13. How should the owner report field-cured versus lab-cured specimens and multiple test ages (7/28/56 day), and which will the official weight, for a material whose strength keeps climbing?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [METHOD]

14. How does an owner design the actual experiment, a mix matrix / design-of-experiments varying cement percentage, moisture, and compaction against strength and cost, so the "tested scientifically to pass the press" claim is real and reproducible?
Lands in: Testing, Commissioning, and Quality Control. [TEST-TO-PROVE] [METHOD] [CREATIVE-IDEA]

## Business and Brand Framework

### A. Audience, Channel, and Content-Brand Mechanics

1. What do YouTube's current (2026) monetization thresholds, ad-revenue share, and policy-compliance requirements state exactly, verified against YouTube's own current help pages rather than third-party estimators?
Lands in: Business and Brand Framework. [REAL-WORLD-EXPERIENCE] [COST]
Leads: support.google.com/youtube help pages; existing chapter already has 2026 tier figures from Wave 2, re-verify currency.

2. What real, creator-disclosed (not third-party-estimated) income reports exist from documented owner-builder or homestead-construction channels or blogs, and what do they show about the actual timeline from first post to any meaningful revenue?
Lands in: Business and Brand Framework. [REAL-WORLD-EXPERIENCE]
Leads: search for individual creators' own "income report" posts rather than aggregator estimates.

3. What is the actual current (2026) RPM/CPM range for the homesteading, off-grid, and DIY-construction YouTube niches, from a source better than a marketing listicle?
Lands in: Business and Brand Framework. [COST]
Leads: outlierkit.com figures (RPM ~$6.90, CPM $10-13) are unverified and need a stronger source before use.

4. What does Faircompanies/Kirsten Dirksen's roughly 20-year operating history show about sustaining an independent building-documentation media brand long-term: format changes, revenue mix, and what kept the audience over that span?
Lands in: Business and Brand Framework. [REAL-WORLD-EXPERIENCE]
Leads: faircompanies.com; direct interview material exists (fieldmag.com Q&A cited in this research).

5. What specific platforms and formats have other solo owner-builders of unconventional homes (earthbag, rammed earth, ICF, concrete) actually used to document their builds, and which formats did they later say they regretted or would change?
Lands in: Business and Brand Framework. [REAL-WORLD-EXPERIENCE] [PROBLEM-SOLUTION]

6. What does the "delay-post" security practice already specified in the existing chapter cost in audience engagement or monetization, and how do other creators who delay-post for safety reasons handle the tradeoff?

7. What is the realistic minimum viable audience size at which coaching or a paid digital product starts generating meaningful income, per the newsletter-monetization sources that claim coaching does not require a large audience?
